<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every integer <i>d</i> ≥ 3 and inverse temperature <i>β</i> ≥ 0, the free zero-field ferromagnetic Ising measure on the infinite <i>d</i>-regular tree is a factor of IID exactly when $`\tanh\beta\le(d-1)^{-1/2}`$. We prove the positive implication, including equality, resolving the conjecture of Nam, Sly and Zhang; the strict converse is known. The spin factor can be chosen to commute with every tree automorphism on every label input.

- **claim-sharp-fiid-threshold** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-sharp)): For every integer $d\ge3$ and every $\beta\ge0$, the free zero-field Ising law $\mu_{d,\beta}$ is a factor of IID in the sense of <a href="#eq:equivariance" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:equivariance">[eq:equivariance]</a> if and only if $$\tanh\beta\le(d-1)^{-1/2}.$$ In this range the factor can be chosen to commute with every automorphism on every label input.
  - Explanation: The free zero-field Ising law on the regular tree is a factor of IID exactly up to the reconstruction-type threshold, equality included. In that range the factor can be taken equivariant on every label input.
- **claim-posterior-identities** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-posterior)): For a fixed deterministic time $t\ge0$, set $$m_v(t)=\mathbb E[\sigma_v\mid\mathcal F_t^0],\qquad m_{v\to w}(t) =\mathbb E[\sigma_v\mid\mathcal F_t^{T_{v\to w}}].$$ These means lie in $(-1,1)$ almost surely. Write $h_v=\operatorname{atanh}m_v$ and $h_{v\to w}=\operatorname{atanh}m_{v\to w}$, and define $$\psi(z)=\operatorname{atanh}(\theta\tanh z),\qquad a(z)=\frac{1-\tanh^2z}{1-\theta^2\tanh^2z}.$$ At each fixed time, almost surely, $$\label{eq:cavity} \begin{aligned} h_{v\to w}(t) &=X_v(t)+\sum_{\substack{u\sim v\\u\ne w}} \psi(h_{u\to v}(t)),\\ h_v(t) &=X_v(t)+\sum_{u\sim v}\psi(h_{u\to v}(t)) =h_{v\to w}(t)+\psi(h_{w\to v}(t)). \end{aligned}$$ The fields have causal, progressively measurable Borel versions as functions of the observation paths. The component version uses only paths in $T_{v\to w}$. These versions require no distinguished root and satisfy, at every input and time, $$\label{eq:site-field-bound} \|h_v(t)-X_v(t)\|\le d\beta,\qquad \|h_{v\to w}(t)-X_v(t)\|\le b\beta.$$ In the process formulas below, use these chosen field versions and set $m_v(t)=\tanh h_v(t)$ and $m_{v\to w}(t)=\tanh h_{v\to w}(t)$. For the simple path $v=v_0,v_1,\ldots,v_r=u$, define $$\label{eq:covariance} \begin{aligned} c_{vu}(t) &=\theta^r\prod_{i=1}^r a(h_{v_i\to v_{i-1}}(t)),& c_{vv}(t)&=1,\\ C_{vu}(t)&=(1-m_v(t)^2)c_{vu}(t). \end{aligned}$$ The last expression is a progressively measurable version of $\mathop{\mathrm{Cov}}(\sigma_v,\sigma_u\mid\mathcal F_t^0)$ at each fixed time. The cavity and covariance formulas also hold in every finite connected subtree, with branches restricted to that subtree. For all real $z$, $$0<a(z)\le1,\qquad \|\psi(z)\|\le\beta,\qquad \psi'(z)=\theta a(z)\in(0,\theta],$$ and $0\le C_{vu}(t)\le\theta^{\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(v,u)}$ at each fixed time.
  - Explanation: Gives the cavity recursion for site and component posterior fields, with causal Borel versions, uniform deviation bounds from the raw observation, and the path-product formula for the response coefficients and conditional covariance.
- **claim-binary-fusion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-binary-fusion)): Let $\varepsilon$ be a uniform sign and let two random elements of standard Borel spaces be independent conditional on $\varepsilon$. Let $U,V$ be the posterior means of $\varepsilon$ from the respective signals. Suppose that their marginal laws are symmetric and $\|U\|,\|V\|<1$ almost surely. If $\mathbb E_{\mathrm{prod}}$ denotes expectation under the product of these two marginal laws, then the posterior mean from both signals is $(U+V)/(1+UV)$, and its second moment is $$\label{eq:binary-fusion} \mathbb EU^2+\mathbb EV^2- \mathbb E_{\mathrm{prod}} \frac{U^2V^2(2-U^2-V^2)}{1-U^2V^2}.$$ In particular this second moment is at most $\mathbb EU^2+\mathbb EV^2$. If $\|U\|,\|V\|\le\theta$, the subtracted term is at least $2(1-\theta^2)\mathbb EU^2\,\mathbb EV^2$.
  - Explanation: Computes the second moment of the combined posterior mean of two conditionally independent symmetric signals and shows a definite loss relative to the sum of the separate second moments.
- **claim-critical-information** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-critical-information)): There are constants $c,C>0$, depending only on $d$, such that for every $t>0$ and every vertex $v$, $$\label{eq:critical-information} c\omega(t)\le M(t)\le C\omega(t), \qquad \mathbb Em_v(t)^2\le C\omega(t).$$ One may take $c=1/5$. In particular both upper bounds are $C\sqrt t$ for $0<t\le1$.
  - Explanation: At the critical parameter the second moment of a component posterior mean is of the order of the small-time scale, from above and below; the whole-site second moment has the same upper bound.
- **claim-component-spin-tilts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-component-spin-tilts)): For every integrable function $f(Y)$ and $\eta\in\{-1,1\}$, $$\label{eq:component-field-tilts} \begin{aligned} \mathbb E[f(Y)\mid\sigma_v=\eta] &=\mathbb E[f(Y)(1+\eta\tanh Y)],\\ \mathbb E[f(Y)\mid\sigma_w=\eta] &=\mathbb E[f(Y)(1+\eta\theta\tanh Y)]. \end{aligned}$$ In particular either conditioning leaves the expectation of every integrable even function of $Y$ unchanged.
  - Explanation: Conditioning a component field on an adjacent spin multiplies its law by a bounded linear tilt, leaving expectations of even functions unchanged.
- **claim-path-attenuation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-path-attenuation)): There is $c_0>0$, depending only on $d$, such that for every $t>0$, every $z\in\mathbb R$, and $\eta\in\{-1,1\}$, $$\label{eq:shifted-attenuation} \mathbb E\left[ a\bigl(z+\psi(h_{v\to w}(t))\bigr)^2 \,\middle\|\,\sigma_w=\eta\right] \le1-c_0\omega(t).$$ For every deterministic simple path $v_0,\ldots,v_r$, $r\ge1$, $$\label{eq:conditional-path-attenuation} \mathbb E\left[ \prod_{i=1}^r a(h_{v_i\to v_{i-1}}(t))^2 \,\middle\|\,\sigma_{v_0},\ldots,\sigma_{v_r}\right] \le e^{-c_0\omega(t)r}.$$ The same bound holds for the reverse product $\prod_{i=1}^r a(h_{v_{i-1}\to v_i}(t))^2$, and both bounds also hold without conditioning on the path spins.
  - Explanation: Intact infinite side branches make squared path products of response factors decay exponentially in the path length on the scale set by the small-time information bound, in both orientations.
- **claim-row-energy** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-row-energy)): For every vertex $v$, there is $C<\infty$, depending only on $d$ at the critical parameter, such that $$\label{eq:row-energy} \begin{aligned} A_v(t)&\le1+\frac{d\theta^2}{1-b\theta^2} &&\text{if }b\theta^2<1,\quad t\ge0,\\ \mathbb EA_v(t)&\le\frac{C}{\omega(t)} &&\text{if }b\theta^2=1,\quad t>0,\\ \mathbb E\int_0^T A_v(t)\,\mathrm dt&<\infty &&\text{for every }T<\infty\text{ in either case}. \end{aligned}$$ The same fixed-time and integrated upper bounds hold with $\sum_u C_{vu}(t)^2$ in place of $A_v(t)$.
  - Explanation: The squared response row is bounded below the threshold, is of inverse small-time order in expectation at the critical parameter, and is time-integrable on every finite horizon.
- **claim-infinite-innovation-equations** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-innovations)): In the planted observation law $X_v(t)=t\sigma_v+B_v(t)$, every finite subvector of $(W_v)_{v\in\mathcal V}$ is a standard vector Brownian motion in both the raw observation filtration $(\mathcal F_t^0)$ and its usual augmentation $(\mathcal F_t)$. In particular $W$ has product Wiener law.* *The posterior means have continuous versions, starting at zero, and $$\label{eq:magnetization-sde} m_v(t)=\sum_{u\in\mathcal V}\int_0^t C_{vu}(s)\,\mathrm dW_u(s).$$ At each fixed time these versions are $\mathbb E[\sigma_v\mid\mathcal F_t]$, and $C_{vu}$ is the covariance conditional on $\mathcal F_t$. The site fields and the component fields can be chosen as jointly Borel, progressively measurable functions of time and observation history that are continuous in time almost surely. For every input, the chosen function $h_{v\to w}(t)$ uses only observations in $T_{v\to w}$ through time $t$. Almost surely the cavity identities and the bounds <a href="#eq:site-field-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:site-field-bound">[eq:site-field-bound]</a> then hold simultaneously at all times and all their indices.* *With the response coefficients $c_{vu}$ from <a href="#eq:covariance" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:covariance">[eq:covariance]</a> and $A_v=\sum_u c_{vu}^2$ from <a href="#eq:row-energy" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:row-energy">[eq:row-energy]</a>, the continuous site field satisfies the equation below. In every integral, $A_v$ is assigned value zero wherever this sum is infinite. $$\label{eq:field-sde} h_v(t)=\sum_{u\in\mathcal V}\int_0^t c_{vu}(s)\,\mathrm dW_u(s) +\int_0^t m_v(s)A_v(s)\,\mathrm ds.$$ Each Brownian sum in this proposition converges, along any exhaustion by finite vertex sets, in the norm $$\\|Z\\|_{\mathcal M_T^2} =\left(\mathbb E\sup_{0\le t\le T}\|Z(t)\|^2\right)^{1/2} \qquad(T<\infty).$$ The drift in <a href="#eq:field-sde" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:field-sde">[eq:field-sde]</a> is absolutely integrable on each such horizon almost surely and in expectation.
  - Explanation: The innovations are independent Brownian motions in both observation filtrations, and the posterior mean and site field satisfy infinite stochastic equations driven by them, with convergent Brownian sums and integrable drift.
- **claim-continuous-augmentation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-continuous-augmentation)): If $Y$ is a continuous square-integrable martingale in $(\mathcal G_t^0)$ and is continuous in $L^2$ at each time, then it is also a martingale in $(\mathcal G_t)$. More generally, if $Z$ is integrable and a version $M(t)=\mathbb E[Z\mid\mathcal G_t^0]$ is continuous in $L^1$, then $M(t)=\mathbb E[Z\mid\mathcal G_t]$ at every fixed time.
  - Explanation: A raw-filtration martingale continuous in the square-integrable sense stays a martingale in the usual augmentation, and the analogous statement holds for conditional expectations continuous in the integrable sense.
- **claim-finite-posterior-energy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-finite-energy)): Let $D$ be a finite connected subtree. For $u\in D$ put $$W_u^D(t)=X_u(t)-\int_0^t m_u^D(s)\,\mathrm ds.$$ The vector $W^D$ is standard Brownian motion in the usual augmentation of $(\mathcal F_t^D)$, and, for $v\in D$, $$\label{eq:finite-energy} \begin{split} m_v^D(t)&=\sum_{u\in D}\int_0^t C_{vu}^D(s)\,\mathrm dW_u^D(s),\\ \mathbb E[m_v^D(t)^2] &=\mathbb E\int_0^t\sum_{u\in D}C_{vu}^D(s)^2\,\mathrm ds . \end{split}$$
  - Explanation: On a finite subtree the finite-observation innovations are Brownian, the posterior mean is a stochastic integral against them, and its second moment equals the integrated squared covariance row.
- **claim-gaussian-path-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-gaussian-path-tail)): For every $\delta>0$ there is $\kappa_\delta\in(0,1)$ such that, for every finite connected subtree $D$, every $v,u\in D$, and every $s\ge\delta$, $$\label{eq:gaussian-path-tail} \mathbb E[C_{vu}^D(s)^2] \le\theta^{2r}(1-\kappa_\delta)^r, \qquad r=\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(v,u).$$ The same inequality holds for the whole-tree covariance $C_{vu}(s)$.
  - Explanation: Away from time zero the vertices' own Gaussian noise gives each path step a uniform strict loss, including at leaves, for finite subtrees and for the whole tree.
- **claim-observation-step-approximation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-observation-step-approximation)): For fixed $v,u$ and $T<\infty$, each of $C_{vu}$ and $c_{vu}$ is the $L^2([0,T]\times\Omega,\,\mathrm ds\,\,\mathrm d\mathbb P)$ limit of bounded elementary processes $$G(s)=\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}\xi_j\mathbf1_{(t_j,t_{j+1}]}(s), \qquad 0=t_0<\cdots<t_N=T,$$ where $\xi_j$ is a bounded Borel function of finitely many observations at time $t_j$. In particular each coefficient has a predictable representative for stochastic integration.
  - Explanation: Covariance and response coefficients are limits of bounded elementary processes built from finitely many observations, so they have predictable representatives usable as stochastic integrands.
- **claim-compatible-copies** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-compatible-copies)): The law <a href="#eq:copy-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:copy-law">[eq:copy-law]</a> is invariant under the simultaneous action of each fixed automorphism of $T_d$. Every finite subvector of $W$ is standard vector Brownian motion in $(\mathcal H_t)$. For each copy $j$, both equations <a href="#eq:magnetization-sde" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:magnetization-sde">[eq:magnetization-sde]</a> and <a href="#eq:field-sde" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:field-sde">[eq:field-sde]</a> hold in this filtration, with the coefficients of that copy and the common Brownian motions $W$. Their infinite Brownian sums converge in the maximal $L^2$ norm on every finite time interval.
  - Explanation: Two observation copies independent given the whole innovation array still have Brownian innovations in the joined filtration, and each copy satisfies its own posterior and field equations driven by the common Brownian motions; the joining is automorphism invariant.
- **claim-copy-variance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-copy-variance)): The function $V$ is absolutely continuous on every finite interval. For almost every $t$, $$\label{eq:variance} V'(t)=\sum_{u\in\mathcal V}\mathbb E[(\Delta c_{ou}(t))^2] +2\mathbb E[\Delta h_o(t)(m_o(t)A_o(t)-m'_o(t)A'_o(t))].$$
  - Explanation: The variance of the field discrepancy between the two copies is absolutely continuous and its derivative is a noise term plus a drift coupling term.
- **claim-strict-uniqueness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-strict-uniqueness)): If $b\theta^2<1$, then $V(t)=0$ for every $t\ge0$.
  - Explanation: Below the threshold the discrepancy variance obeys a linear differential inequality and so vanishes identically by Gronwall.
- **claim-path-variance** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-path-variance)): Let $d\ge3$, $b=d-1$, and $0<\theta\le b^{-1/2}$. Let $\pi$ be a probability law on $\mathsf X^2$ with both marginals equal to $\nu$, invariant under the simultaneous action of $\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(T_d)$. Suppose that $g=(g_e)$ assigns a measurable real function of $\mathsf X$ to every directed edge, with $\mathbb E_\nu g_e^2<\infty$, and that for each fixed $\gamma\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(T_d)$ and directed edge $e$, $$g_{\gamma e}(\gamma x)=g_e(x) \quad\text{for \(\nu\)-almost every \(x\)}.$$ Here $\gamma(i,j)=(\gamma i,\gamma j)$. For the two coordinate arrays $X,X'$ under $\pi$, set $$D_e=g_e(X)-g_e(X'),\qquad S=\mathbb E_\pi D_e^2.$$ The value of $S$ is independent of the directed edge. There is a constant $C_d<\infty$, depending only on $d$, such that for every integer $r\ge1$ and every simple path $v_0,\ldots,v_r$, $$\label{eq:path-variance} \begin{aligned} \mathbb E_\pi\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^rD_{(v_{i-1},v_i)}\right)^2\right] &\le C_d rS,\\ \mathbb E_\pi\left[\left(\sum_{i=1}^rD_{(v_i,v_{i-1})}\right)^2\right] &\le C_d rS. \end{aligned}$$ In particular, take $\pi$ to be the $(X,X')$-marginal of the joining in Proposition <a href="#prop:compatible-copies" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:compatible-copies">13</a>. Then this holds for the differences $D_e(t)$ in <a href="#eq:log-discrepancy-def" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:log-discrepancy-def">[eq:log-discrepancy-def]</a> at each fixed finite time, with $S=S(t)$.
  - Explanation: The variance of a sum of edge differences along a simple path grows only linearly in the path length, in both edge orientations, for any invariant joining of two copies of the observation law.
- **claim-marginal-vertex-spectrum** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-marginal-spectrum)): For the full observation law $\nu$, the spectrum of $\mathsf A_\nu$ on centered vertex assignments is contained in $[-\rho,\rho]$, where $$\label{eq:spectrum-rho} \rho=\frac{2\sqrt b}{d}<1.$$
  - Explanation: The neighbor-average operator on centered equivariant vertex assignments has spectrum inside an interval of radius strictly less than one.
- **claim-cut-continuation** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cut-continuation)): Let $y$ and $p$ be adjacent vertices, and root the component $\mathcal T_y=T_{y\to p}$ at $y$. Write $\operatorname{Ch}(w)$ for the children of $w$ in this component. For a descendant path $y=w_0,w_1,\ldots,w_L$ of length $L\ge1$, set $H_i=h_{w_i\to w_{i+1}}$ for $0\le i<L$ and define $$\begin{aligned} \widetilde H_0 &=X_y+\sum_{u\in\operatorname{Ch}(y)\setminus\{w_1\}} \psi(h_{u\to y}),\notag\\ \widetilde H_i &=X_{w_i}+\psi(\widetilde H_{i-1}) +\sum_{u\in\operatorname{Ch}(w_i)\setminus\{w_{i+1}\}} \psi(h_{u\to w_i}),\qquad 1\le i<L. \label{eq:inward-cut-recursion} \end{aligned}$$ Each $\widetilde H_i$ is a function only of the observations in $\mathcal T_y$. With $$C_{\mathrm{cut}}=\exp\!\left(\frac{4\beta}{1-\theta}\right),$$ the original and modified inward products obey, almost surely, $$\left\|\log \frac{\prod_{i=0}^{L-1}a(H_i)^2} {\prod_{i=0}^{L-1}a(\widetilde H_i)^2}\right\| \le\frac{4\beta}{1-\theta}. \label{eq:path-cut-cost}$$ Moreover, conditional on the true spins of this path, both the outward product and the modified inward product satisfy $$\begin{aligned} \mathbb E\left[\prod_{i=1}^{L}a(h_{w_i\to w_{i-1}})^2 \,\middle\|\,\sigma_{w_0},\ldots,\sigma_{w_L}\right] &\le e^{-c_0\omega(t)L},\\ \mathbb E\left[\prod_{i=0}^{L-1}a(\widetilde H_i)^2 \,\middle\|\,\sigma_{w_0},\ldots,\sigma_{w_L}\right] &\le e^{-c_0\omega(t)L}. \end{aligned} \label{eq:cut-continuation-attenuation}$$ The constants are independent of $t$, $L$, and the choice of path.
  - Explanation: Removing the single message entering across a cut makes an inward product depend only on descendant observations at bounded multiplicative cost, and both continuation products attenuate exponentially in the length.
- **claim-path-average-concentration** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-path-average)): Set $$\eta=\min\left\{\frac{c_0}{4},\frac{\log b}{8}\right\},\qquad C_0=\max\left\{2,\frac{4\log2}{c_0}\right\},\qquad \ell_*(t)=\left\lceil\frac{C_0}{\omega(t)}\right\rceil.$$ For every $t>0$, every vertex $z$, every allowed first-step restriction $F$, either orientation $\varepsilon\in\{+,-\}$, and every integer $\ell\ge\ell_*(t)$, $$\mathbb P\left(G^\varepsilon_\ell(z;F) >C_{\mathrm{cut}}e^{-\eta\omega(t)\ell}\right) \le\exp\left(-\frac{b^{\ell/4}}{2b}\right). \label{eq:double-exp}$$ The constants depend only on $d$ and are uniform in the time, vertex, first-step restriction, and orientation.
  - Explanation: Averages of squared path products over many continuations concentrate with doubly exponential probability beyond a length threshold, in either orientation.
- **claim-weighted-path-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-weighted-paths)): For every finite $T>0$ there are $c,C>0$, depending only on $d$, and $C_T<\infty$, depending also on $T$, such that for $0<t\le T$ and every integer $r\ge2\ell_*(t)$, $$\begin{aligned} Q_r(t)&\le C e^{-c\omega(t)r}\,rS(t) +C_T r^2\exp(-c b^{cr}),\\ L_r(t)&\le C e^{-c\omega(t)r}\,V(t) +C_T\exp(-c b^{cr}). \end{aligned} \label{eq:weighted-paths}$$ At every integer $r\ge1$, without a lower length restriction, $$Q_r(t)\le2C_{\mathrm{var}}rS(t),\qquad L_r(t)\le V(t), \label{eq:unweighted-bounds}$$ where $C_{\mathrm{var}}$ is the constant in Proposition <a href="#prop:path-variance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:path-variance">16</a>. The hypothesis for <a href="#eq:weighted-paths" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weighted-paths">[eq:weighted-paths]</a> follows in particular from $r\ge2(C_0+1)/\omega(t)$.
  - Explanation: Path sums weighted by the discrepancy and its variance obey exponentially attenuated bounds for long paths and unweighted bounds for all lengths, even though weights depend on the same observations.
- **claim-critical-field-moments-tail** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-critical-tail)): There are constants $0<K<\infty$ and $t_0\in(0,1)$, depending only on $d$, such that for every integer $p\ge1$ and every $0<t\le t_0$, $$\mathbb E\|h_{v\to w}(t)\|^p\le e^{Kp^2}t^{p/4}. \label{eq:critical-all-moments}$$ There are also $c>0$ and $t_{\mathrm{tail}}\in(0,t_0]$ such that $$\mathbb P\bigl(\|h_{v\to w}(t)\|>t^{1/8}\bigr) \le \exp\bigl(-c\log^2(1/t)\bigr), \qquad 0<t\le t_{\mathrm{tail}}. \label{eq:critical-field-tail}$$ Both inequalities hold as well when conditioned on either value of the component-root spin $\sigma_v$, or on either value of the deleted-neighbor spin $\sigma_w$.
  - Explanation: At the critical parameter all integer moments of the component field are controlled at small times, giving a tail estimate for the field exceeding a small power of the time.
- **claim-small-log-loss** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-small-log-loss)): There is a fixed $t_*\in(0,\min\{t_{\mathrm{tail}},e^{-1}\}]$ such that, for $0<t\le\min\{t_*,T\}$ with $V(t)>0$, $$S(t)\le C_T V(t) \left(t^{1/4}+\frac{I(t)}{\log^2(1/t)}\right). \label{eq:small-log-loss}$$ At every time in $[0,T]$, the bound $S(t)\le CV(t)$ remains valid.
  - Explanation: At small times the variance of the log-factor difference is smaller than the site discrepancy variance by an improvement coming from the vanishing derivative of the log factor at zero.
- **claim-cutoff-sums** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-cutoff-sums)): At each time $0<t\le T$ with $V(t)>0$, $$\begin{split} \sum_{r\ge1}Q_r &\le C_T\left[S\bigl(\omega^{-2}+(\log I)^2\bigr)+V^2\right],\\ \sum_{r\ge1}\sqrt{Q_r} &\le C_T\left[\sqrt S\bigl(\omega^{-3/2} +(\log I)^{3/2}\bigr)+V\right],\\ \sum_{r\ge1}L_r &\le C_T\left[V\bigl(\omega^{-1}+\log I\bigr)+V^2\right]. \end{split} \label{eq:cutoff-sums}$$
  - Explanation: The noise, square-root drift and weighted path sums are bounded using a path-length cutoff that depends on the discrepancy.
- **claim-critical-uniqueness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-critical-uniqueness)): For the invariant conditional coupling with common innovations at $\theta=b^{-1/2}$, the site-field discrepancy satisfies $V(t)=0$ for every $t\ge0$.
  - Explanation: At the critical parameter the site-field discrepancy of the conditional copies vanishes identically, by an integrable Osgood-type comparison.
- **claim-pointwise-spin-factor** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-pointwise-spin-factor)): Let $(U_v)_{v\in\mathcal V}$ be independent uniform labels on $T_d$. Suppose a Borel map $\Phi:[0,1]^{\mathcal V}\to\{-1,1\}^{\mathcal V}$ satisfies $\Phi(gU)=g\Phi(U)$ almost surely for each fixed $g\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(T_d)$. There is a Borel map $\Psi:[0,1]^{\mathcal V}\to\{-1,1\}^{\mathcal V}$ such that $\Psi(U)=\Phi(U)$ almost surely and $$\Psi(gx)=g\Psi(x) \qquad\text{for every }x\in[0,1]^{\mathcal V} \text{ and }g\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(T_d).$$
  - Explanation: An almost-surely equivariant spin factor is modified on a null set, by averaging local conditional expectations over finite ball automorphism groups, into one equivariant on every input.
- **claim-factor-extraction** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-factor-extraction)): Fix $d\ge3$ and $0<\theta=\tanh\beta\le(d-1)^{-1/2}$, and retain the path spaces and laws $\mathsf Q,\mathsf w,\mathsf K$ defined in Section <a href="#sec:coupling" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:coupling">5</a>. There is a Borel map $F:\mathsf W\to\mathsf X$ such that $$\mathsf K(w,\cdot)=\delta_{F(w)} \quad\text{for $\mathsf w$-almost every }w, \qquad X=F(W)\quad\mathsf Q\text{-almost surely}.$$ For each fixed $g\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(T_d)$, $F(gW)=gF(W)$ almost surely. The free Ising law is the law of a Borel map of vertex IID uniform labels which commutes with every automorphism on every label input. The same spin-factor conclusion holds at $\theta=0$.
  - Explanation: Equal conditional copies make the conditional law of the observations a point mass, giving a Borel recovery map from innovations to observations; spins are read from integer-time slopes and composed with a Brownian sampler to give the equivariant spin factor.

      "exact_statement": "For a fixed deterministic time $t\\ge0$, set $$m_v(t)=\\mathbb E[\\sigma_v\\mid\\mathcal F_t^0],\\qquad\n m_{v\\to w}(t)\n   =\\mathbb E[\\sigma_v\\mid\\mathcal F_t^{T_{v\\to w}}].$$ These means lie in $(-1,1)$ almost surely. Write $h_v=\\operatorname{atanh}m_v$ and $h_{v\\to w}=\\operatorname{atanh}m_{v\\to w}$, and define $$\\psi(z)=\\operatorname{atanh}(\\theta\\tanh z),\\qquad\n a(z)=\\frac{1-\\tanh^2z}{1-\\theta^2\\tanh^2z}.$$ At each fixed time, almost surely, $$\\label{eq:cavity}\n \\begin{aligned}\n h_{v\\to w}(t)\n   &=X_v(t)+\\sum_{\\substack{u\\sim v\\\\u\\ne w}}\n                       \\psi(h_{u\\to v}(t)),\\\\\n h_v(t)\n   &=X_v(t)+\\sum_{u\\sim v}\\psi(h_{u\\to v}(t))\n     =h_{v\\to w}(t)+\\psi(h_{w\\to v}(t)).\n \\end{aligned}$$ The fields have causal, progressively measurable Borel versions as functions of the observation paths. The component version uses only paths in $T_{v\\to w}$. These versions require no distinguished root and satisfy, at every input and time, $$\\label{eq:site-field-bound}\n |h_v(t)-X_v(t)|\\le d\\beta,\\qquad\n |h_{v\\to w}(t)-X_v(t)|\\le b\\beta.$$ In the process formulas below, use these chosen field versions and set $m_v(t)=\\tanh h_v(t)$ and $m_{v\\to w}(t)=\\tanh h_{v\\to w}(t)$. For the simple path $v=v_0,v_1,\\ldots,v_r=u$, define $$\\label{eq:covariance}\n \\begin{aligned}\n c_{vu}(t)\n   &=\\theta^r\\prod_{i=1}^r\n                  a(h_{v_i\\to v_{i-1}}(t)),&\n c_{vv}(t)&=1,\\\\\n C_{vu}(t)&=(1-m_v(t)^2)c_{vu}(t).\n \\end{aligned}$$ The last expression is a progressively measurable version of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Cov}}(\\sigma_v,\\sigma_u\\mid\\mathcal F_t^0)$ at each fixed time. The cavity and covariance formulas also hold in every finite connected subtree, with branches restricted to that subtree. For all real $z$, $$0<a(z)\\le1,\\qquad |\\psi(z)|\\le\\beta,\\qquad\n \\psi'(z)=\\theta a(z)\\in(0,\\theta],$$ and $0\\le C_{vu}(t)\\le\\theta^{\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(v,u)}$ at each fixed time.",
      "explanation": "Gives the cavity recursion for site and component posterior fields, with causal Borel versions, uniform deviation bounds from the raw observation, and the path-product formula for the response coefficients and conditional covariance.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-binary-fusion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\varepsilon$ be a uniform sign and let two random elements of standard Borel spaces be independent conditional on $\\varepsilon$. Let $U,V$ be the posterior means of $\\varepsilon$ from the respective signals. Suppose that their marginal laws are symmetric and $|U|,|V|<1$ almost surely. If $\\mathbb E_{\\mathrm{prod}}$ denotes expectation under the product of these two marginal laws, then the posterior mean from both signals is $(U+V)/(1+UV)$, and its second moment is $$\\label{eq:binary-fusion}\n \\mathbb EU^2+\\mathbb EV^2-\n \\mathbb E_{\\mathrm{prod}}\n   \\frac{U^2V^2(2-U^2-V^2)}{1-U^2V^2}.$$ In particular this second moment is at most $\\mathbb EU^2+\\mathbb EV^2$. If $|U|,|V|\\le\\theta$, the subtracted term is at least $2(1-\\theta^2)\\mathbb EU^2\\,\\mathbb EV^2$.",
        "source_id": "PM-236.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-binary-fusion",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-component-spin-tilts",
        "exact_quote": "For every integrable function $f(Y)$ and $\\eta\\in\\{-1,1\\}$, $$\\label{eq:component-field-tilts}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E[f(Y)\\mid\\sigma_v=\\eta]\n   &=\\mathbb E[f(Y)(1+\\eta\\tanh Y)],\\\\\n \\mathbb E[f(Y)\\mid\\sigma_w=\\eta]\n   &=\\mathbb E[f(Y)(1+\\eta\\theta\\tanh Y)].\n \\end{aligned}$$ In particular either conditioning leaves the expectation of every integrable even function of $Y$ unchanged.",
        "source_id": "PM-236.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-component-spin-tilts",
          "exact_quote": "For every integrable function $f(Y)$ and $\\eta\\in\\{-1,1\\}$, $$\\label{eq:component-field-tilts}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E[f(Y)\\mid\\sigma_v=\\eta]\n   &=\\mathbb E[f(Y)(1+\\eta\\tanh Y)],\\\\\n \\mathbb E[f(Y)\\mid\\sigma_w=\\eta]\n   &=\\mathbb E[f(Y)(1+\\eta\\theta\\tanh Y)].\n \\end{aligned}$$ In particular either conditioning leaves the expectation of every integrable even function of $Y$ unchanged.",
          "source_id": "PM-236.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "For every integrable function $f(Y)$ and $\\eta\\in\\{-1,1\\}$, $$\\label{eq:component-field-tilts}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E[f(Y)\\mid\\sigma_v=\\eta]\n   &=\\mathbb E[f(Y)(1+\\eta\\tanh Y)],\\\\\n \\mathbb E[f(Y)\\mid\\sigma_w=\\eta]\n   &=\\mathbb E[f(Y)(1+\\eta\\theta\\tanh Y)].\n \\end{aligned}$$ In particular either conditioning leaves the expectation of every integrable even function of $Y$ unchanged.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-path-attenuation",
        "exact_quote": "There is $c_0>0$, depending only on $d$, such that for every $t>0$, every $z\\in\\mathbb R$, and $\\eta\\in\\{-1,1\\}$, $$\\label{eq:shifted-attenuation}\n \\mathbb E\\left[\n   a\\bigl(z+\\psi(h_{v\\to w}(t))\\bigr)^2\n       \\,\\middle|\\,\\sigma_w=\\eta\\right]\n \\le1-c_0\\omega(t).$$ For every deterministic simple path $v_0,\\ldots,v_r$, $r\\ge1$, $$\\label{eq:conditional-path-attenuation}\n \\mathbb E\\left[\n   \\prod_{i=1}^r a(h_{v_i\\to v_{i-1}}(t))^2\n       \\,\\middle|\\,\\sigma_{v_0},\\ldots,\\sigma_{v_r}\\right]\n \\le e^{-c_0\\omega(t)r}.$$ The same bound holds for the reverse product $\\prod_{i=1}^r a(h_{v_{i-1}\\to v_i}(t))^2$, and both bounds also hold without conditioning on the path spins.",
        "source_id": "PM-236.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-path-attenuation",
          "exact_quote": "There is $c_0>0$, depending only on $d$, such that for every $t>0$, every $z\\in\\mathbb R$, and $\\eta\\in\\{-1,1\\}$, $$\\label{eq:shifted-attenuation}\n \\mathbb E\\left[\n   a\\bigl(z+\\psi(h_{v\\to w}(t))\\bigr)^2\n       \\,\\middle|\\,\\sigma_w=\\eta\\right]\n \\le1-c_0\\omega(t).$$ For every deterministic simple path $v_0,\\ldots,v_r$, $r\\ge1$, $$\\label{eq:conditional-path-attenuation}\n \\mathbb E\\left[\n   \\prod_{i=1}^r a(h_{v_i\\to v_{i-1}}(t))^2\n       \\,\\middle|\\,\\sigma_{v_0},\\ldots,\\sigma_{v_r}\\right]\n \\le e^{-c_0\\omega(t)r}.$$ The same bound holds for the reverse product $\\prod_{i=1}^r a(h_{v_{i-1}\\to v_i}(t))^2$, and both bounds also hold without conditioning on the path spins.",
          "source_id": "PM-236.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "There is $c_0>0$, depending only on $d$, such that for every $t>0$, every $z\\in\\mathbb R$, and $\\eta\\in\\{-1,1\\}$, $$\\label{eq:shifted-attenuation}\n \\mathbb E\\left[\n   a\\bigl(z+\\psi(h_{v\\to w}(t))\\bigr)^2\n       \\,\\middle|\\,\\sigma_w=\\eta\\right]\n \\le1-c_0\\omega(t).$$ For every deterministic simple path $v_0,\\ldots,v_r$, $r\\ge1$, $$\\label{eq:conditional-path-attenuation}\n \\mathbb E\\left[\n   \\prod_{i=1}^r a(h_{v_i\\to v_{i-1}}(t))^2\n       \\,\\middle|\\,\\sigma_{v_0},\\ldots,\\sigma_{v_r}\\right]\n \\le e^{-c_0\\omega(t)r}.$$ The same bound holds for the reverse product $\\prod_{i=1}^r a(h_{v_{i-1}\\to v_i}(t))^2$, and both bounds also hold without conditioning on the path spins.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-row-energy",
        "exact_quote": "For every vertex $v$, there is $C<\\infty$, depending only on $d$ at the critical parameter, such that $$\\label{eq:row-energy}\n \\begin{aligned}\n A_v(t)&\\le1+\\frac{d\\theta^2}{1-b\\theta^2}\n      &&\\text{if }b\\theta^2<1,\\quad t\\ge0,\\\\\n \\mathbb EA_v(t)&\\le\\frac{C}{\\omega(t)}\n      &&\\text{if }b\\theta^2=1,\\quad t>0,\\\\\n \\mathbb E\\int_0^T A_v(t)\\,\\mathrm dt&<\\infty\n      &&\\text{for every }T<\\infty\\text{ in either case}.\n \\end{aligned}$$ The same fixed-time and integrated upper bounds hold with $\\sum_u C_{vu}(t)^2$ in place of $A_v(t)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-236.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-row-energy",
          "exact_quote": "For every vertex $v$, there is $C<\\infty$, depending only on $d$ at the critical parameter, such that $$\\label{eq:row-energy}\n \\begin{aligned}\n A_v(t)&\\le1+\\frac{d\\theta^2}{1-b\\theta^2}\n      &&\\text{if }b\\theta^2<1,\\quad t\\ge0,\\\\\n \\mathbb EA_v(t)&\\le\\frac{C}{\\omega(t)}\n      &&\\text{if }b\\theta^2=1,\\quad t>0,\\\\\n \\mathbb E\\int_0^T A_v(t)\\,\\mathrm dt&<\\infty\n      &&\\text{for every }T<\\infty\\text{ in either case}.\n \\end{aligned}$$ The same fixed-time and integrated upper bounds hold with $\\sum_u C_{vu}(t)^2$ in place of $A_v(t)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-236.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every vertex $v$, there is $C<\\infty$, depending only on $d$ at the critical parameter, such that $$\\label{eq:row-energy}\n \\begin{aligned}\n A_v(t)&\\le1+\\frac{d\\theta^2}{1-b\\theta^2}\n      &&\\text{if }b\\theta^2<1,\\quad t\\ge0,\\\\\n \\mathbb EA_v(t)&\\le\\frac{C}{\\omega(t)}\n      &&\\text{if }b\\theta^2=1,\\quad t>0,\\\\\n \\mathbb E\\int_0^T A_v(t)\\,\\mathrm dt&<\\infty\n      &&\\text{for every }T<\\infty\\text{ in either case}.\n \\end{aligned}$$ The same fixed-time and integrated upper bounds hold with $\\sum_u C_{vu}(t)^2$ in place of $A_v(t)$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-innovations",
        "exact_quote": "In the planted observation law $X_v(t)=t\\sigma_v+B_v(t)$, every finite subvector of $(W_v)_{v\\in\\mathcal V}$ is a standard vector Brownian motion in both the raw observation filtration $(\\mathcal F_t^0)$ and its usual augmentation $(\\mathcal F_t)$. In particular $W$ has product Wiener law.*\n\n\n*The posterior means have continuous versions, starting at zero, and $$\\label{eq:magnetization-sde}\n m_v(t)=\\sum_{u\\in\\mathcal V}\\int_0^t C_{vu}(s)\\,\\mathrm dW_u(s).$$ At each fixed time these versions are $\\mathbb E[\\sigma_v\\mid\\mathcal F_t]$, and $C_{vu}$ is the covariance conditional on $\\mathcal F_t$. The site fields and the component fields can be chosen as jointly Borel, progressively measurable functions of time and observation history that are continuous in time almost surely. For every input, the chosen function $h_{v\\to w}(t)$ uses only observations in $T_{v\\to w}$ through time $t$. Almost surely the cavity identities and the bounds <a href=\"#eq:site-field-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:site-field-bound\">[eq:site-field-bound]</a> then hold simultaneously at all times and all their indices.*\n\n\n*With the response coefficients $c_{vu}$ from <a href=\"#eq:covariance\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:covariance\">[eq:covariance]</a> and $A_v=\\sum_u c_{vu}^2$ from <a href=\"#eq:row-energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:row-energy\">[eq:row-energy]</a>, the continuous site field satisfies the equation below. In every integral, $A_v$ is assigned value zero wherever this sum is infinite. $$\\label{eq:field-sde}\n h_v(t)=\\sum_{u\\in\\mathcal V}\\int_0^t c_{vu}(s)\\,\\mathrm dW_u(s)\n            +\\int_0^t m_v(s)A_v(s)\\,\\mathrm ds.$$ Each Brownian sum in this proposition converges, along any exhaustion by finite vertex sets, in the norm $$\\|Z\\|_{\\mathcal M_T^2}\n       =\\left(\\mathbb E\\sup_{0\\le t\\le T}|Z(t)|^2\\right)^{1/2}\n       \\qquad(T<\\infty).$$ The drift in <a href=\"#eq:field-sde\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:field-sde\">[eq:field-sde]</a> is absolutely integrable on each such horizon almost surely and in expectation.",
        "source_id": "PM-236.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-innovations",
          "exact_quote": "In the planted observation law $X_v(t)=t\\sigma_v+B_v(t)$, every finite subvector of $(W_v)_{v\\in\\mathcal V}$ is a standard vector Brownian motion in both the raw observation filtration $(\\mathcal F_t^0)$ and its usual augmentation $(\\mathcal F_t)$. In particular $W$ has product Wiener law.*\n\n\n*The posterior means have continuous versions, starting at zero, and $$\\label{eq:magnetization-sde}\n m_v(t)=\\sum_{u\\in\\mathcal V}\\int_0^t C_{vu}(s)\\,\\mathrm dW_u(s).$$ At each fixed time these versions are $\\mathbb E[\\sigma_v\\mid\\mathcal F_t]$, and $C_{vu}$ is the covariance conditional on $\\mathcal F_t$. The site fields and the component fields can be chosen as jointly Borel, progressively measurable functions of time and observation history that are continuous in time almost surely. For every input, the chosen function $h_{v\\to w}(t)$ uses only observations in $T_{v\\to w}$ through time $t$. Almost surely the cavity identities and the bounds <a href=\"#eq:site-field-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:site-field-bound\">[eq:site-field-bound]</a> then hold simultaneously at all times and all their indices.*\n\n\n*With the response coefficients $c_{vu}$ from <a href=\"#eq:covariance\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:covariance\">[eq:covariance]</a> and $A_v=\\sum_u c_{vu}^2$ from <a href=\"#eq:row-energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:row-energy\">[eq:row-energy]</a>, the continuous site field satisfies the equation below. In every integral, $A_v$ is assigned value zero wherever this sum is infinite. $$\\label{eq:field-sde}\n h_v(t)=\\sum_{u\\in\\mathcal V}\\int_0^t c_{vu}(s)\\,\\mathrm dW_u(s)\n            +\\int_0^t m_v(s)A_v(s)\\,\\mathrm ds.$$ Each Brownian sum in this proposition converges, along any exhaustion by finite vertex sets, in the norm $$\\|Z\\|_{\\mathcal M_T^2}\n       =\\left(\\mathbb E\\sup_{0\\le t\\le T}|Z(t)|^2\\right)^{1/2}\n       \\qquad(T<\\infty).$$ The drift in <a href=\"#eq:field-sde\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:field-sde\">[eq:field-sde]</a> is absolutely integrable on each such horizon almost surely and in expectation.",
          "source_id": "PM-236.1"

      "exact_statement": "In the planted observation law $X_v(t)=t\\sigma_v+B_v(t)$, every finite subvector of $(W_v)_{v\\in\\mathcal V}$ is a standard vector Brownian motion in both the raw observation filtration $(\\mathcal F_t^0)$ and its usual augmentation $(\\mathcal F_t)$. In particular $W$ has product Wiener law.*\n\n\n*The posterior means have continuous versions, starting at zero, and $$\\label{eq:magnetization-sde}\n m_v(t)=\\sum_{u\\in\\mathcal V}\\int_0^t C_{vu}(s)\\,\\mathrm dW_u(s).$$ At each fixed time these versions are $\\mathbb E[\\sigma_v\\mid\\mathcal F_t]$, and $C_{vu}$ is the covariance conditional on $\\mathcal F_t$. The site fields and the component fields can be chosen as jointly Borel, progressively measurable functions of time and observation history that are continuous in time almost surely. For every input, the chosen function $h_{v\\to w}(t)$ uses only observations in $T_{v\\to w}$ through time $t$. Almost surely the cavity identities and the bounds <a href=\"#eq:site-field-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:site-field-bound\">[eq:site-field-bound]</a> then hold simultaneously at all times and all their indices.*\n\n\n*With the response coefficients $c_{vu}$ from <a href=\"#eq:covariance\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:covariance\">[eq:covariance]</a> and $A_v=\\sum_u c_{vu}^2$ from <a href=\"#eq:row-energy\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:row-energy\">[eq:row-energy]</a>, the continuous site field satisfies the equation below. In every integral, $A_v$ is assigned value zero wherever this sum is infinite. $$\\label{eq:field-sde}\n h_v(t)=\\sum_{u\\in\\mathcal V}\\int_0^t c_{vu}(s)\\,\\mathrm dW_u(s)\n            +\\int_0^t m_v(s)A_v(s)\\,\\mathrm ds.$$ Each Brownian sum in this proposition converges, along any exhaustion by finite vertex sets, in the norm $$\\|Z\\|_{\\mathcal M_T^2}\n       =\\left(\\mathbb E\\sup_{0\\le t\\le T}|Z(t)|^2\\right)^{1/2}\n       \\qquad(T<\\infty).$$ The drift in <a href=\"#eq:field-sde\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:field-sde\">[eq:field-sde]</a> is absolutely integrable on each such horizon almost surely and in expectation.",
      "explanation": "The innovations are independent Brownian motions in both observation filtrations, and the posterior mean and site field satisfy infinite stochastic equations driven by them, with convergent Brownian sums and integrable drift.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-continuous-augmentation",
        "exact_quote": "If $Y$ is a continuous square-integrable martingale in $(\\mathcal G_t^0)$ and is continuous in $L^2$ at each time, then it is also a martingale in $(\\mathcal G_t)$. More generally, if $Z$ is integrable and a version $M(t)=\\mathbb E[Z\\mid\\mathcal G_t^0]$ is continuous in $L^1$, then $M(t)=\\mathbb E[Z\\mid\\mathcal G_t]$ at every fixed time.",
        "source_id": "PM-236.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-continuous-augmentation",
          "exact_quote": "If $Y$ is a continuous square-integrable martingale in $(\\mathcal G_t^0)$ and is continuous in $L^2$ at each time, then it is also a martingale in $(\\mathcal G_t)$.           "source_id": "PM-236.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "If $Y$ is a continuous square-integrable martingale in $(\\mathcal G_t^0)$ and is continuous in $L^2$ at each time, then it is also a martingale in $(\\mathcal G_t)$.       "explanation": "A raw-filtration martingale continuous in the square-integrable sense stays a martingale in the usual augmentation, and the analogous statement holds for conditional expectations continuous in the integrable sense.",

      "hypotheses": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-energy",
        "exact_quote": "Let $D$ be a finite connected subtree. For $u\\in D$ put $$W_u^D(t)=X_u(t)-\\int_0^t m_u^D(s)\\,\\mathrm ds.$$ The vector $W^D$ is standard Brownian motion in the usual augmentation of $(\\mathcal F_t^D)$, and, for $v\\in D$, $$\\label{eq:finite-energy}\n \\begin{split}\n m_v^D(t)&=\\sum_{u\\in D}\\int_0^t C_{vu}^D(s)\\,\\mathrm dW_u^D(s),\\\\\n \\mathbb E[m_v^D(t)^2]\n   &=\\mathbb E\\int_0^t\\sum_{u\\in D}C_{vu}^D(s)^2\\,\\mathrm ds .\n \\end{split}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-236.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-finite-energy",
          "exact_quote": "Let $D$ be a finite connected subtree.           "source_id": "PM-236.1"
        }
      ],

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-gaussian-path-tail",
        "exact_quote": "For every $\\delta>0$ there is $\\kappa_\\delta\\in(0,1)$ such that, for every finite connected subtree $D$, every $v,u\\in D$, and every $s\\ge\\delta$, $$\\label{eq:gaussian-path-tail}\n \\mathbb E[C_{vu}^D(s)^2]\n     \\le\\theta^{2r}(1-\\kappa_\\delta)^r,\n       \\qquad r=\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(v,u).$$ The same inequality holds for the whole-tree covariance $C_{vu}(s)$.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-compatible-copies",
        "exact_quote": "The law <a href=\"#eq:copy-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:copy-law\">[eq:copy-law]</a> is invariant under the simultaneous action of each fixed automorphism of $T_d$. For each copy $j$, both equations <a href=\"#eq:magnetization-sde\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:magnetization-sde\">[eq:magnetization-sde]</a> and <a href=\"#eq:field-sde\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:field-sde\">[eq:field-sde]</a> hold in this filtration, with the coefficients of that copy and the common Brownian motions $W$.       },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-path-variance",
        "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge3$, $b=d-1$, and $0<\\theta\\le b^{-1/2}$. Let $\\pi$ be a probability law on $\\mathsf X^2$ with both marginals equal to $\\nu$, invariant under the simultaneous action of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Aut}}(T_d)$. Suppose that $g=(g_e)$ assigns a measurable real function of $\\mathsf X$ to every directed edge, with $\\mathbb E_\\nu g_e^2<\\infty$, and that for each fixed $\\gamma\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Aut}}(T_d)$ and directed edge $e$, $$g_{\\gamma e}(\\gamma x)=g_e(x)\n \\quad\\text{for \\(\\nu\\)-almost every \\(x\\)}.$$ Here $\\gamma(i,j)=(\\gamma i,\\gamma j)$. For the two coordinate arrays $X,X'$ under $\\pi$, set $$D_e=g_e(X)-g_e(X'),\\qquad S=\\mathbb E_\\pi D_e^2.$$ The value of $S$ is independent of the directed edge. There is a constant $C_d<\\infty$, depending only on $d$, such that for every integer $r\\ge1$ and every simple path $v_0,\\ldots,v_r$, $$\\label{eq:path-variance}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E_\\pi\\left[\\left(\\sum_{i=1}^rD_{(v_{i-1},v_i)}\\right)^2\\right]\n     &\\le C_d rS,\\\\\n \\mathbb E_\\pi\\left[\\left(\\sum_{i=1}^rD_{(v_i,v_{i-1})}\\right)^2\\right]\n     &\\le C_d rS.\n \\end{aligned}$$ In particular, take $\\pi$ to be the $(X,X')$-marginal of the joining in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:compatible-copies\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:compatible-copies\">13</a>. Then this holds for the differences $D_e(t)$ in <a href=\"#eq:log-discrepancy-def\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:log-discrepancy-def\">[eq:log-discrepancy-def]</a> at each fixed finite time, with $S=S(t)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-236.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-path-variance",
          "exact_quote": "Let $d\\ge3$, $b=d-1$, and $0<\\theta\\le b^{-1/2}$. Let $\\pi$ be a probability law on $\\mathsf X^2$ with both marginals equal to $\\nu$, invariant under the simultaneous action of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Aut}}(T_d)$. Suppose that $g=(g_e)$ assigns a measurable real function of $\\mathsf X$ to every directed edge, with $\\mathbb E_\\nu g_e^2<\\infty$, and that for each fixed $\\gamma\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Aut}}(T_d)$ and directed edge $e$, $$g_{\\gamma e}(\\gamma x)=g_e(x)\n \\quad\\text{for \\(\\nu\\)-almost every \\(x\\)}.$$ Here $\\gamma(i,j)=(\\gamma i,\\gamma j)$. For the two coordinate arrays $X,X'$ under $\\pi$, set $$D_e=g_e(X)-g_e(X'),\\qquad S=\\mathbb E_\\pi D_e^2.$$ The value of $S$ is independent of the directed edge. There is a constant $C_d<\\infty$, depending only on $d$, such that for every integer $r\\ge1$ and every simple path $v_0,\\ldots,v_r$, $$\\label{eq:path-variance}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E_\\pi\\left[\\left(\\sum_{i=1}^rD_{(v_{i-1},v_i)}\\right)^2\\right]\n     &\\le C_d rS,\\\\\n \\mathbb E_\\pi\\left[\\left(\\sum_{i=1}^rD_{(v_i,v_{i-1})}\\right)^2\\right]\n     &\\le C_d rS.\n \\end{aligned}$$ In particular, take $\\pi$ to be the $(X,X')$-marginal of the joining in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:compatible-copies\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:compatible-copies\">13</a>. Then this holds for the differences $D_e(t)$ in <a href=\"#eq:log-discrepancy-def\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:log-discrepancy-def\">[eq:log-discrepancy-def]</a> at each fixed finite time, with $S=S(t)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-236.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $d\\ge3$, $b=d-1$, and $0<\\theta\\le b^{-1/2}$. Let $\\pi$ be a probability law on $\\mathsf X^2$ with both marginals equal to $\\nu$, invariant under the simultaneous action of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Aut}}(T_d)$. Suppose that $g=(g_e)$ assigns a measurable real function of $\\mathsf X$ to every directed edge, with $\\mathbb E_\\nu g_e^2<\\infty$, and that for each fixed $\\gamma\\in\\mathop{\\mathrm{Aut}}(T_d)$ and directed edge $e$, $$g_{\\gamma e}(\\gamma x)=g_e(x)\n \\quad\\text{for \\(\\nu\\)-almost every \\(x\\)}.$$ Here $\\gamma(i,j)=(\\gamma i,\\gamma j)$. For the two coordinate arrays $X,X'$ under $\\pi$, set $$D_e=g_e(X)-g_e(X'),\\qquad S=\\mathbb E_\\pi D_e^2.$$ The value of $S$ is independent of the directed edge. There is a constant $C_d<\\infty$, depending only on $d$, such that for every integer $r\\ge1$ and every simple path $v_0,\\ldots,v_r$, $$\\label{eq:path-variance}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E_\\pi\\left[\\left(\\sum_{i=1}^rD_{(v_{i-1},v_i)}\\right)^2\\right]\n     &\\le C_d rS,\\\\\n \\mathbb E_\\pi\\left[\\left(\\sum_{i=1}^rD_{(v_i,v_{i-1})}\\right)^2\\right]\n     &\\le C_d rS.\n \\end{aligned}$$ In particular, take $\\pi$ to be the $(X,X')$-marginal of the joining in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:compatible-copies\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:compatible-copies\">13</a>. Then this holds for the differences $D_e(t)$ in <a href=\"#eq:log-discrepancy-def\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:log-discrepancy-def\">[eq:log-discrepancy-def]</a> at each fixed finite time, with $S=S(t)$.",
      "explanation": "The variance of a sum of edge differences along a simple path grows only linearly in the path length, in both edge orientations, for any invariant joining of two copies of the observation law.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cut-continuation",
        "exact_quote": "Let $y$ and $p$ be adjacent vertices, and root the component $\\mathcal T_y=T_{y\\to p}$ at $y$. Write $\\operatorname{Ch}(w)$ for the children of $w$ in this component. For a descendant path $y=w_0,w_1,\\ldots,w_L$ of length $L\\ge1$, set $H_i=h_{w_i\\to w_{i+1}}$ for $0\\le i<L$ and define $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\widetilde H_0\n  &=X_y+\\sum_{u\\in\\operatorname{Ch}(y)\\setminus\\{w_1\\}}\n                   \\psi(h_{u\\to y}),\\notag\\\\\n \\widetilde H_i\n  &=X_{w_i}+\\psi(\\widetilde H_{i-1})\n       +\\sum_{u\\in\\operatorname{Ch}(w_i)\\setminus\\{w_{i+1}\\}}\n                   \\psi(h_{u\\to w_i}),\\qquad 1\\le i<L.\n \\label{eq:inward-cut-recursion}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Each $\\widetilde H_i$ is a function only of the observations in $\\mathcal T_y$. With $$C_{\\mathrm{cut}}=\\exp\\!\\left(\\frac{4\\beta}{1-\\theta}\\right),$$ the original and modified inward products obey, almost surely, $$\\left|\\log\n   \\frac{\\prod_{i=0}^{L-1}a(H_i)^2}\n        {\\prod_{i=0}^{L-1}a(\\widetilde H_i)^2}\\right|\n       \\le\\frac{4\\beta}{1-\\theta}.\n \\label{eq:path-cut-cost}$$ Moreover, conditional on the true spins of this path, both the outward product and the modified inward product satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E\\left[\\prod_{i=1}^{L}a(h_{w_i\\to w_{i-1}})^2\n       \\,\\middle|\\,\\sigma_{w_0},\\ldots,\\sigma_{w_L}\\right]\n       &\\le e^{-c_0\\omega(t)L},\\\\\n \\mathbb E\\left[\\prod_{i=0}^{L-1}a(\\widetilde H_i)^2\n       \\,\\middle|\\,\\sigma_{w_0},\\ldots,\\sigma_{w_L}\\right]\n       &\\le e^{-c_0\\omega(t)L}.\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{eq:cut-continuation-attenuation}$$ The constants are independent of $t$, $L$, and the choice of path.",
        "source_id": "PM-236.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-cut-continuation",
          "exact_quote": "Let $y$ and $p$ be adjacent vertices, and root the component $\\mathcal T_y=T_{y\\to p}$ at $y$. Write $\\operatorname{Ch}(w)$ for the children of $w$ in this component. For a descendant path $y=w_0,w_1,\\ldots,w_L$ of length $L\\ge1$, set $H_i=h_{w_i\\to w_{i+1}}$ for $0\\le i<L$ and define $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\widetilde H_0\n  &=X_y+\\sum_{u\\in\\operatorname{Ch}(y)\\setminus\\{w_1\\}}\n                   \\psi(h_{u\\to y}),\\notag\\\\\n \\widetilde H_i\n  &=X_{w_i}+\\psi(\\widetilde H_{i-1})\n       +\\sum_{u\\in\\operatorname{Ch}(w_i)\\setminus\\{w_{i+1}\\}}\n                   \\psi(h_{u\\to w_i}),\\qquad 1\\le i<L.\n \\label{eq:inward-cut-recursion}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Each $\\widetilde H_i$ is a function only of the observations in $\\mathcal T_y$. With $$C_{\\mathrm{cut}}=\\exp\\!\\left(\\frac{4\\beta}{1-\\theta}\\right),$$ the original and modified inward products obey, almost surely, $$\\left|\\log\n   \\frac{\\prod_{i=0}^{L-1}a(H_i)^2}\n        {\\prod_{i=0}^{L-1}a(\\widetilde H_i)^2}\\right|\n       \\le\\frac{4\\beta}{1-\\theta}.\n \\label{eq:path-cut-cost}$$ Moreover, conditional on the true spins of this path, both the outward product and the modified inward product satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E\\left[\\prod_{i=1}^{L}a(h_{w_i\\to w_{i-1}})^2\n       \\,\\middle|\\,\\sigma_{w_0},\\ldots,\\sigma_{w_L}\\right]\n       &\\le e^{-c_0\\omega(t)L},\\\\\n \\mathbb E\\left[\\prod_{i=0}^{L-1}a(\\widetilde H_i)^2\n       \\,\\middle|\\,\\sigma_{w_0},\\ldots,\\sigma_{w_L}\\right]\n       &\\le e^{-c_0\\omega(t)L}.\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{eq:cut-continuation-attenuation}$$ The constants are independent of $t$, $L$, and the choice of path.",
          "source_id": "PM-236.1"

      "exact_statement": "Let $y$ and $p$ be adjacent vertices, and root the component $\\mathcal T_y=T_{y\\to p}$ at $y$. Write $\\operatorname{Ch}(w)$ for the children of $w$ in this component. For a descendant path $y=w_0,w_1,\\ldots,w_L$ of length $L\\ge1$, set $H_i=h_{w_i\\to w_{i+1}}$ for $0\\le i<L$ and define $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\widetilde H_0\n  &=X_y+\\sum_{u\\in\\operatorname{Ch}(y)\\setminus\\{w_1\\}}\n                   \\psi(h_{u\\to y}),\\notag\\\\\n \\widetilde H_i\n  &=X_{w_i}+\\psi(\\widetilde H_{i-1})\n       +\\sum_{u\\in\\operatorname{Ch}(w_i)\\setminus\\{w_{i+1}\\}}\n                   \\psi(h_{u\\to w_i}),\\qquad 1\\le i<L.\n \\label{eq:inward-cut-recursion}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Each $\\widetilde H_i$ is a function only of the observations in $\\mathcal T_y$. With $$C_{\\mathrm{cut}}=\\exp\\!\\left(\\frac{4\\beta}{1-\\theta}\\right),$$ the original and modified inward products obey, almost surely, $$\\left|\\log\n   \\frac{\\prod_{i=0}^{L-1}a(H_i)^2}\n        {\\prod_{i=0}^{L-1}a(\\widetilde H_i)^2}\\right|\n       \\le\\frac{4\\beta}{1-\\theta}.\n \\label{eq:path-cut-cost}$$ Moreover, conditional on the true spins of this path, both the outward product and the modified inward product satisfy $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E\\left[\\prod_{i=1}^{L}a(h_{w_i\\to w_{i-1}})^2\n       \\,\\middle|\\,\\sigma_{w_0},\\ldots,\\sigma_{w_L}\\right]\n       &\\le e^{-c_0\\omega(t)L},\\\\\n \\mathbb E\\left[\\prod_{i=0}^{L-1}a(\\widetilde H_i)^2\n       \\,\\middle|\\,\\sigma_{w_0},\\ldots,\\sigma_{w_L}\\right]\n       &\\le e^{-c_0\\omega(t)L}.\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{eq:cut-continuation-attenuation}$$ The constants are independent of $t$, $L$, and the choice of path.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-path-average",
        "exact_quote": "Set $$\\eta=\\min\\left\\{\\frac{c_0}{4},\\frac{\\log b}{8}\\right\\},\\qquad\n C_0=\\max\\left\\{2,\\frac{4\\log2}{c_0}\\right\\},\\qquad\n \\ell_*(t)=\\left\\lceil\\frac{C_0}{\\omega(t)}\\right\\rceil.$$ For every $t>0$, every vertex $z$, every allowed first-step restriction $F$, either orientation $\\varepsilon\\in\\{+,-\\}$, and every integer $\\ell\\ge\\ell_*(t)$, $$\\mathbb P\\left(G^\\varepsilon_\\ell(z;F)\n       >C_{\\mathrm{cut}}e^{-\\eta\\omega(t)\\ell}\\right)\n       \\le\\exp\\left(-\\frac{b^{\\ell/4}}{2b}\\right).\n \\label{eq:double-exp}$$ The constants depend only on $d$ and are uniform in the time, vertex, first-step restriction, and orientation.",
        "source_id": "PM-236.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-path-average",
          "exact_quote": "Set $$\\eta=\\min\\left\\{\\frac{c_0}{4},\\frac{\\log b}{8}\\right\\},\\qquad\n C_0=\\max\\left\\{2,\\frac{4\\log2}{c_0}\\right\\},\\qquad\n \\ell_*(t)=\\left\\lceil\\frac{C_0}{\\omega(t)}\\right\\rceil.$$ For every $t>0$, every vertex $z$, every allowed first-step restriction $F$, either orientation $\\varepsilon\\in\\{+,-\\}$, and every integer $\\ell\\ge\\ell_*(t)$, $$\\mathbb P\\left(G^\\varepsilon_\\ell(z;F)\n       >C_{\\mathrm{cut}}e^{-\\eta\\omega(t)\\ell}\\right)\n       \\le\\exp\\left(-\\frac{b^{\\ell/4}}{2b}\\right).\n \\label{eq:double-exp}$$ The constants depend only on $d$ and are uniform in the time, vertex, first-step restriction, and orientation.",
          "source_id": "PM-236.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Set $$\\eta=\\min\\left\\{\\frac{c_0}{4},\\frac{\\log b}{8}\\right\\},\\qquad\n C_0=\\max\\left\\{2,\\frac{4\\log2}{c_0}\\right\\},\\qquad\n \\ell_*(t)=\\left\\lceil\\frac{C_0}{\\omega(t)}\\right\\rceil.$$ For every $t>0$, every vertex $z$, every allowed first-step restriction $F$, either orientation $\\varepsilon\\in\\{+,-\\}$, and every integer $\\ell\\ge\\ell_*(t)$, $$\\mathbb P\\left(G^\\varepsilon_\\ell(z;F)\n       >C_{\\mathrm{cut}}e^{-\\eta\\omega(t)\\ell}\\right)\n       \\le\\exp\\left(-\\frac{b^{\\ell/4}}{2b}\\right).\n \\label{eq:double-exp}$$ The constants depend only on $d$ and are uniform in the time, vertex, first-step restriction, and orientation.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-weighted-paths",
        "exact_quote": "For every finite $T>0$ there are $c,C>0$, depending only on $d$, and $C_T<\\infty$, depending also on $T$, such that for $0<t\\le T$ and every integer $r\\ge2\\ell_*(t)$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n Q_r(t)&\\le C e^{-c\\omega(t)r}\\,rS(t)\n       +C_T r^2\\exp(-c b^{cr}),\\\\\n L_r(t)&\\le C e^{-c\\omega(t)r}\\,V(t)\n       +C_T\\exp(-c b^{cr}).\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{eq:weighted-paths}$$ At every integer $r\\ge1$, without a lower length restriction, $$Q_r(t)\\le2C_{\\mathrm{var}}rS(t),\\qquad L_r(t)\\le V(t),\n \\label{eq:unweighted-bounds}$$ where $C_{\\mathrm{var}}$ is the constant in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:path-variance\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:path-variance\">16</a>. The hypothesis for <a href=\"#eq:weighted-paths\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:weighted-paths\">[eq:weighted-paths]</a> follows in particular from $r\\ge2(C_0+1)/\\omega(t)$.",
        "source_id": "PM-236.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-weighted-paths",
          "exact_quote": "For every finite $T>0$ there are $c,C>0$, depending only on $d$, and $C_T<\\infty$, depending also on $T$, such that for $0<t\\le T$ and every integer $r\\ge2\\ell_*(t)$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n Q_r(t)&\\le C e^{-c\\omega(t)r}\\,rS(t)\n       +C_T r^2\\exp(-c b^{cr}),\\\\\n L_r(t)&\\le C e^{-c\\omega(t)r}\\,V(t)\n       +C_T\\exp(-c b^{cr}).\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{eq:weighted-paths}$$ At every integer $r\\ge1$, without a lower length restriction, $$Q_r(t)\\le2C_{\\mathrm{var}}rS(t),\\qquad L_r(t)\\le V(t),\n \\label{eq:unweighted-bounds}$$ where $C_{\\mathrm{var}}$ is the constant in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:path-variance\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:path-variance\">16</a>. The hypothesis for <a href=\"#eq:weighted-paths\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:weighted-paths\">[eq:weighted-paths]</a> follows in particular from $r\\ge2(C_0+1)/\\omega(t)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-236.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "For every finite $T>0$ there are $c,C>0$, depending only on $d$, and $C_T<\\infty$, depending also on $T$, such that for $0<t\\le T$ and every integer $r\\ge2\\ell_*(t)$, $$\\begin{aligned}\n Q_r(t)&\\le C e^{-c\\omega(t)r}\\,rS(t)\n       +C_T r^2\\exp(-c b^{cr}),\\\\\n L_r(t)&\\le C e^{-c\\omega(t)r}\\,V(t)\n       +C_T\\exp(-c b^{cr}).\n \\end{aligned}\n \\label{eq:weighted-paths}$$ At every integer $r\\ge1$, without a lower length restriction, $$Q_r(t)\\le2C_{\\mathrm{var}}rS(t),\\qquad L_r(t)\\le V(t),\n \\label{eq:unweighted-bounds}$$ where $C_{\\mathrm{var}}$ is the constant in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:path-variance\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:path-variance\">16</a>. The hypothesis for <a href=\"#eq:weighted-paths\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:weighted-paths\">[eq:weighted-paths]</a> follows in particular from $r\\ge2(C_0+1)/\\omega(t)$.",
      "explanation": "Path sums weighted by the discrepancy and its variance obey exponentially attenuated bounds for long paths and unweighted bounds for all lengths, even though weights depend on the same observations.",

The hypothesis for <a href=\"#eq:weighted-paths\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:weighted-paths\">[eq:weighted-paths]</a> follows in particular from $r\\ge2(C_0+1)/\\omega(t)$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-critical-tail",
        "exact_quote": "There are constants $0<K<\\infty$ and $t_0\\in(0,1)$, depending only on $d$, such that for every integer $p\\ge1$ and every $0<t\\le t_0$, $$\\mathbb E|h_{v\\to w}(t)|^p\\le e^{Kp^2}t^{p/4}.\n \\label{eq:critical-all-moments}$$ There are also $c>0$ and $t_{\\mathrm{tail}}\\in(0,t_0]$ such that $$\\mathbb P\\bigl(|h_{v\\to w}(t)|>t^{1/8}\\bigr)\n \\le \\exp\\bigl(-c\\log^2(1/t)\\bigr),\n \\qquad 0<t\\le t_{\\mathrm{tail}}.\n \\label{eq:critical-field-tail}$$ Both inequalities hold as well when conditioned on either value of the component-root spin $\\sigma_v$, or on either value of the deleted-neighbor spin $\\sigma_w$.",
        "source_id": "PM-236.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-critical-tail",
          "exact_quote": "There are constants $0<K<\\infty$ and $t_0\\in(0,1)$, depending only on $d$, such that for every integer $p\\ge1$ and every $0<t\\le t_0$, $$\\mathbb E|h_{v\\to w}(t)|^p\\le e^{Kp^2}t^{p/4}.\n \\label{eq:critical-all-moments}$$ There are also $c>0$ and $t_{\\mathrm{tail}}\\in(0,t_0]$ such that $$\\mathbb P\\bigl(|h_{v\\to w}(t)|>t^{1/8}\\bigr)\n \\le \\exp\\bigl(-c\\log^2(1/t)\\bigr),\n \\qquad 0<t\\le t_{\\mathrm{tail}}.\n \\label{eq:critical-field-tail}$$ Both inequalities hold as well when conditioned on either value of the component-root spin $\\sigma_v$, or on either value of the deleted-neighbor spin $\\sigma_w$.",
          "source_id": "PM-236.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There are constants $0<K<\\infty$ and $t_0\\in(0,1)$, depending only on $d$, such that for every integer $p\\ge1$ and every $0<t\\le t_0$, $$\\mathbb E|h_{v\\to w}(t)|^p\\le e^{Kp^2}t^{p/4}.\n \\label{eq:critical-all-moments}$$ There are also $c>0$ and $t_{\\mathrm{tail}}\\in(0,t_0]$ such that $$\\mathbb P\\bigl(|h_{v\\to w}(t)|>t^{1/8}\\bigr)\n \\le \\exp\\bigl(-c\\log^2(1/t)\\bigr),\n \\qquad 0<t\\le t_{\\mathrm{tail}}.\n \\label{eq:critical-field-tail}$$ Both inequalities hold as well when conditioned on either value of the component-root spin $\\sigma_v$, or on either value of the deleted-neighbor spin $\\sigma_w$.",
      "explanation": "At the critical parameter all integer moments of the component field are controlled at small times, giving a tail estimate for the field exceeding a small power of the time.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-small-log-loss",
        "exact_quote": "There is a fixed $t_*\\in(0,\\min\\{t_{\\mathrm{tail}},e^{-1}\\}]$ such that, for $0<t\\le\\min\\{t_*,T\\}$ with $V(t)>0$, $$S(t)\\le C_T V(t)\n   \\left(t^{1/4}+\\frac{I(t)}{\\log^2(1/t)}\\right).\n \\label{eq:small-log-loss}$$ At every time in $[0,T]$, the bound $S(t)\\le CV(t)$ remains valid.",
        "source_id": "PM-236.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-small-log-loss",
          "exact_quote": "There is a fixed $t_*\\in(0,\\min\\{t_{\\mathrm{tail}},e^{-1}\\}]$ such that, for $0<t\\le\\min\\{t_*,T\\}$ with $V(t)>0$, $$S(t)\\le C_T V(t)\n   \\left(t^{1/4}+\\frac{I(t)}{\\log^2(1/t)}\\right).\n \\label{eq:small-log-loss}$$ At every time in $[0,T]$, the bound $S(t)\\le CV(t)$ remains valid.",

      "exact_statement": "There is a fixed $t_*\\in(0,\\min\\{t_{\\mathrm{tail}},e^{-1}\\}]$ such that, for $0<t\\le\\min\\{t_*,T\\}$ with $V(t)>0$, $$S(t)\\le C_T V(t)\n   \\left(t^{1/4}+\\frac{I(t)}{\\log^2(1/t)}\\right).\n \\label{eq:small-log-loss}$$ At every time in $[0,T]$, the bound $S(t)\\le CV(t)$ remains valid.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-cutoff-sums",
        "exact_quote": "At each time $0<t\\le T$ with $V(t)>0$, $$\\begin{split}\n \\sum_{r\\ge1}Q_r\n &\\le C_T\\left[S\\bigl(\\omega^{-2}+(\\log I)^2\\bigr)+V^2\\right],\\\\\n \\sum_{r\\ge1}\\sqrt{Q_r}\n &\\le C_T\\left[\\sqrt S\\bigl(\\omega^{-3/2}\n                          +(\\log I)^{3/2}\\bigr)+V\\right],\\\\\n \\sum_{r\\ge1}L_r\n &\\le C_T\\left[V\\bigl(\\omega^{-1}+\\log I\\bigr)+V^2\\right].\n \\end{split}\n \\label{eq:cutoff-sums}$$",
        "source_id": "PM-236.1"
      },
      "evidence": [

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **factor of IID** ([source](#eq:equivariance)): We call a spin law a *factor of IID* if it is the law of $\Phi(U)$ for independent uniform labels $(U_v)_{v\in\mathcal V}$ and a measurable map $\Phi:[0,1]^{\mathcal V}\to\{-1,1\}^{\mathcal V}$ satisfying $$\label{eq:equivariance} \Phi(gU)=g\Phi(U)\quad\text{almost surely for each fixed } g\in\mathop{\mathrm{Aut}}(T_d),\qquad (gx)_v=x_{g^{-1}v}.$$ The exceptional null set may depend on $g$. For spin laws this convention is equivalent to requiring a Borel factor equivariant on every input: Lemma <a href="#lem:pointwise-spin-factor" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:pointwise-spin-factor">25</a> constructs such a version. The input contains no distinguished root or end. Measurability does not require a spin to be determined by finitely many labels; no finitary coding or coding-radius estimate is asserted.
- **planted observation process** ([source](#eq:observations)): $$\label{eq:observations} X_v(t)=t\sigma_v+B_v(t),\qquad \mathcal F_t^0 =\sigma\{X_v(s):v\in\mathcal V,\ 0\le s\le t\}.$$ The $B_v$’s are the noise used to plant these observations. The innovations $W_v$, obtained later by subtracting the conditional drift from $X_v$, are different processes. In this section $\mathcal F_t^0$ is the raw observation filtration; we reserve $\mathcal F_t$ for its usual augmentation. For any $D\subseteq\mathcal V$, write $$\mathcal F_t^D =\sigma\{X_u(s):u\in D,\ 0\le s\le t\}.$$
- **innovation** ([source](#eq:innovations-definition)): For each vertex define its *innovation* by $$\label{eq:innovations-definition} W_v(t)=X_v(t)-\int_0^t m_v(s)\,\mathrm ds.$$ The integrand is a bounded, progressively measurable observation function. Thus this formula defines continuous paths for every observation input. Their values through time $t$ use only the observation history through $t$. The time integrals are Borel functions of that history, so $X\mapsto W$ is a causal Borel map on the countable product of continuous path spaces. The centered-ball definitions of the posterior means also show that this map commutes with graph relabeling.
- **conditional-copy law** ([source](#eq:copy-law)): Write $$\begin{gathered} C_0([0,\infty),\mathbb R) =\{x\in C([0,\infty),\mathbb R):x(0)=0\},\\ \mathsf X=\mathsf W=C_0([0,\infty),\mathbb R)^{\mathcal V}. \end{gathered}$$ Give the path space its locally uniform Polish topology and the arrays the countable product topology. The subscript $0$ specifies the value at time zero and imposes no condition at infinity. Let $\mathsf Q$ be the joint law of $(X,W)$ from the planted observation construction. Its $W$-marginal is product Wiener measure, denoted by $\mathsf w$. These are standard Borel spaces, so there is a regular conditional probability $\mathsf K(w,\,\mathrm dx)$ for $X$ given $W=w$. Define a probability law on the three path arrays by $$\label{eq:copy-law} \widehat{\mathsf Q}(\,\mathrm dw,\,\mathrm dx,\,\mathrm dx') =\mathsf w(\,\mathrm dw)\,\mathsf K(w,\,\mathrm dx)\,\mathsf K(w,\,\mathrm dx').$$ Write $X^1,X^2,W$ for its coordinates. Each pair $(X^j,W)$ has law $\mathsf Q$, while $X^1$ and $X^2$ are independent conditional on $W$. The conditioning in <a href="#eq:copy-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:copy-law">[eq:copy-law]</a> uses every coordinate of $W$ at every time, not only the portion already observed.
- **posterior notation for finite subtrees** ([source](#eq:finite-posterior-derivative)): Let $$M_v^D(x)=\sum_\eta\eta_v\pi_x^D(\eta),\qquad m_v^D(t)=M_v^D(X_D(t)),\qquad h_v^D(t)=\operatorname{atanh}m_v^D(t).$$ By Bayes’ formula, $m_v^D(t)$ is a version of $\mathbb E[\sigma_v\mid\mathcal F_t^D]$. All posterior masses are positive, so $\|M_v^D(x)\|<1$ and the field is finite. The functions $M_v^D$ are smooth. If $u,v\in D$, then differentiating the finite sum in <a href="#eq:finite-posterior" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:finite-posterior">[eq:finite-posterior]</a> gives $$\label{eq:finite-posterior-derivative} \frac{\partial M_v^D}{\partial x_u}(x) =\mathop{\mathrm{Cov}}_{\pi_x^D}(\eta_v,\eta_u).$$ At $x=X_D(t)$ we denote this conditional covariance by $C_{vu}^D(t)$.
- **edge log-discrepancy and its variance** ([source](#eq:log-discrepancy-def)): The coefficients in this equation are products along directed paths. We first control the difference of one factor. For a directed edge $e=(i,j)$ define $$\label{eq:log-discrepancy-def} D_e(t)=\log a(h_{j\to i}(t))- \log a(h'_{j\to i}(t)), \qquad S(t)=\mathbb E[D_e(t)^2].$$ Transitivity on directed edges makes $S$ independent of $e$. The two cavity identities at the ends of $ij$ give $$\|\Delta h_{j\to i}\| \le\|\Delta h_j\|+\theta\|\Delta h_{i\to j}\|, \qquad \|\Delta h_{i\to j}\| \le\|\Delta h_i\|+\theta\|\Delta h_{j\to i}\|.$$ Substituting the second inequality into the first yields $$\label{eq:component-difference} \|\Delta h_{j\to i}\| \le\frac{\|\Delta h_j\|+\theta\|\Delta h_i\|}{1-\theta^2}.$$ In particular, on $[0,T]$ it is bounded by $H_T/(1-\theta)$; Minkowski’s inequality and invariance also give $\\|\Delta h_{j\to i}\\|_2\le\sqrt V/(1-\theta)$.
- **weighted path quantities** ([source](#eq:weighted-path-definitions)): For a directed simple path $v_0,\ldots,v_r$, $r\ge1$, let $e_i=(v_{i-1},v_i)$. At a common time $t$, set $$\label{eq:weighted-path-definitions} \begin{aligned} P_r&=\prod_{i=1}^r a(h_{v_i\to v_{i-1}}), &Z_r&=\sum_{i=1}^rD_{e_i},\\ Q_r&=\mathbb E[(P_r^2+(P'_r)^2)Z_r^2], &L_r&=\mathbb E[(\Delta h_{v_0})^2P_r^2]. \end{aligned}$$ The orientation matters: every factor uses the component at the next vertex excluding the preceding one. Invariance makes these expectations depend only on $r$ and $t$, so we suppress the particular path. Both $P_r$ and $P'_r$ lie in $(0,1]$, and $\log P_r-\log P'_r=Z_r$. The mean-value theorem, first for $e^x$ and then for $e^{2x}$, gives $$\label{eq:product-difference} \mathbb E[(P_r-P'_r)^2]\le2Q_r, \qquad \|P_r^2-(P'_r)^2\| \le2(P_r^2+(P'_r)^2)\|Z_r\|.$$ For example, the derivative of $e^x$ on the segment joining $\log P_r$ and $\log P'_r$ is at most $P_r+P'_r$; squaring and using $(P_r+P'_r)^2\le2(P_r^2+(P'_r)^2)$ proves the first bound. The derivative of $e^{2x}$ on the same segment is at most $2(P_r^2+(P'_r)^2)$, proving the second.
- **nonbacktracking and lift operators** ([source](#eq:edge-operator)): A vertex assignment can be read at either endpoint of a directed edge. We compare these two lifts and then isolate the part of an edge assignment orthogonal to both. On the joined assignment spaces define the tail and head lifts $\mathsf F,\mathsf H:\mathcal H_{\mathrm v}(\pi)\to \mathcal H_{\mathrm e}(\pi)$, the reversal $\mathsf J$, and the forward nonbacktracking average $\mathsf N$ by $$\begin{aligned} (\mathsf Ff)_{(i,j)}&=f_i,& (\mathsf Hf)_{(i,j)}&=f_j,& (\mathsf Jg)_{(i,j)}&=g_{(j,i)},\\ (\mathsf Ng)_{(i,j)} &=\frac1b\sum_{\substack{k\sim j\\k\ne i}}g_{(j,k)}. \end{aligned}$$ Invariance makes the two lifts isometries and makes $\mathsf J$ a self-adjoint isometry with $\mathsf J^2=I$. Jensen’s inequality makes $\mathsf N$ a contraction. The adjoints of the lifts are $$\label{eq:lift-adjoints} (\mathsf F^*g)_v=\frac1d\sum_{u\sim v}g_{(v,u)},\qquad (\mathsf H^*g)_v=\frac1d\sum_{u\sim v}g_{(u,v)}.$$ Indeed, in the edge inner product we may average the fixed outgoing edge over all neighbors of its tail, because the tail stabilizer is transitive on those neighbors. This proves the first formula. Interchanging the endpoints first proves the second. In particular, $$\mathsf F^*\mathsf H=\mathsf H^*\mathsf F=\mathsf A_\pi,\qquad \mathsf J\mathsf F=\mathsf H,\qquad \mathsf J\mathsf H=\mathsf F.$$ Direct substitution in the definitions gives $$\begin{aligned} \mathsf N&=\frac db\,\mathsf H\mathsf F^*-\frac1b\,\mathsf J, &\mathsf N^*&=\frac db\,\mathsf F\mathsf H^*-\frac1b\,\mathsf J, \label{eq:edge-operator}\\ \mathsf N\mathsf F&=\mathsf H, &\mathsf N\mathsf H&=\frac db\,\mathsf H\mathsf A_\pi-\frac1b\,\mathsf F. \label{eq:edge-lifts} \end{aligned}$$ Thus $(\mathsf N^*g)_{(i,j)} =b^{-1}\sum_{k\sim i,\ k\ne j}g_{(k,i)}$ and $\mathsf N^*=\mathsf J\mathsf N\mathsf J$.
- **Osgood scale of the discrepancy** ([source](#eq:osgood-size)): $$K_T>\max\{1,H_T^2\},\qquad I(t)=1+\log\frac{K_T}{V(t)}\quad\text{when }V(t)>0. \label{eq:osgood-size}$$ In particular $I(t)>1$, including at times when $V(t)\ge1$.
- **discrepancy-dependent cutoff** ([source](#eq:variance-cutoff)): Choose $C_1$ sufficiently large that $$C_1\ge C_0',\qquad \gamma C_1\log b\ge1, \qquad (\alpha/2)b^{\gamma C_1}\ge2,$$ and take the integer $$R=\left\lceil C_1\bigl(\omega^{-1}+\log I\bigr)\right\rceil. \label{eq:variance-cutoff}$$ The weighted estimates apply for $r>R$. Since $\omega^{-1}\ge1$ and $I>1$, the choices above also give $$(\alpha/2)b^{\gamma r} \ge (\alpha/2)b^{\gamma C_1\omega^{-1}} I^{\gamma C_1\log b} \ge 2I \qquad(r>R).$$ Splitting the exceptional exponent into equal halves therefore gives $$e^{-\alpha b^{\gamma r}} \le e^{-2I}e^{-(\alpha/2)b^{\gamma r}} =e^{-2}\left(\frac{V}{K_T}\right)^2 e^{-(\alpha/2)b^{\gamma r}} \le V^2 e^{-(\alpha/2)b^{\gamma r}}\quad(r>R). \label{eq:exception-absorption}$$ The last step uses $K_T>1$, and so remains valid when $V\ge1$.

          "exact_quote": "Write $$\\begin{gathered}\n C_0([0,\\infty),\\mathbb R)\n   =\\{x\\in C([0,\\infty),\\mathbb R):x(0)=0\\},\\\\\n \\mathsf X=\\mathsf W=C_0([0,\\infty),\\mathbb R)^{\\mathcal V}.\n \\end{gathered}$$ Give the path space its locally uniform Polish topology and the arrays the countable product topology. The subscript $0$ specifies the value at time zero and imposes no condition at infinity. Let $\\mathsf Q$ be the joint law of $(X,W)$ from the planted observation construction. Its $W$-marginal is product Wiener measure, denoted by $\\mathsf w$. These are standard Borel spaces, so there is a regular conditional probability $\\mathsf K(w,\\,\\mathrm dx)$ for $X$ given $W=w$. Define a probability law on the three path arrays by $$\\label{eq:copy-law}\n \\widehat{\\mathsf Q}(\\,\\mathrm dw,\\,\\mathrm dx,\\,\\mathrm dx')\n   =\\mathsf w(\\,\\mathrm dw)\\,\\mathsf K(w,\\,\\mathrm dx)\\,\\mathsf K(w,\\,\\mathrm dx').$$ Write $X^1,X^2,W$ for its coordinates. Each pair $(X^j,W)$ has law $\\mathsf Q$, while $X^1$ and $X^2$ are independent conditional on $W$. The conditioning in <a href=\"#eq:copy-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:copy-law\">[eq:copy-law]</a> uses every coordinate of $W$ at every time, not only the portion already observed.",
          "source_id": "PM-236.1"
        }

          "anchor": "eq:finite-posterior-derivative",
          "exact_quote": "Let $$M_v^D(x)=\\sum_\\eta\\eta_v\\pi_x^D(\\eta),\\qquad\n m_v^D(t)=M_v^D(X_D(t)),\\qquad\n h_v^D(t)=\\operatorname{atanh}m_v^D(t).$$ By Bayes’ formula, $m_v^D(t)$ is a version of $\\mathbb E[\\sigma_v\\mid\\mathcal F_t^D]$. All posterior masses are positive, so $|M_v^D(x)|<1$ and the field is finite. The functions $M_v^D$ are smooth. If $u,v\\in D$, then differentiating the finite sum in <a href=\"#eq:finite-posterior\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:finite-posterior\">[eq:finite-posterior]</a> gives $$\\label{eq:finite-posterior-derivative}\n \\frac{\\partial M_v^D}{\\partial x_u}(x)\n   =\\mathop{\\mathrm{Cov}}_{\\pi_x^D}(\\eta_v,\\eta_u).$$ At $x=X_D(t)$ we denote this conditional covariance by $C_{vu}^D(t)$.",

If $u,v\\in D$, then differentiating the finite sum in <a href=\"#eq:finite-posterior\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:finite-posterior\">[eq:finite-posterior]</a> gives $$\\label{eq:finite-posterior-derivative}\n \\frac{\\partial M_v^D}{\\partial x_u}(x)\n   =\\mathop{\\mathrm{Cov}}_{\\pi_x^D}(\\eta_v,\\eta_u).$$ At $x=X_D(t)$ we denote this conditional covariance by $C_{vu}^D(t)$.",
      "interpretation": "Finite-subtree posterior mean, its time process and its atanh field, whose derivative is the conditional covariance.",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "eq:log-discrepancy-def",
          "exact_quote": "The coefficients in this equation are products along directed paths. We first control the difference of one factor. For a directed edge $e=(i,j)$ define $$\\label{eq:log-discrepancy-def}\n D_e(t)=\\log a(h_{j\\to i}(t))-\n        \\log a(h'_{j\\to i}(t)),\n \\qquad S(t)=\\mathbb E[D_e(t)^2].$$ Transitivity on directed edges makes $S$ independent of $e$. The two cavity identities at the ends of $ij$ give $$|\\Delta h_{j\\to i}|\n     \\le|\\Delta h_j|+\\theta|\\Delta h_{i\\to j}|,\n \\qquad\n |\\Delta h_{i\\to j}|\n     \\le|\\Delta h_i|+\\theta|\\Delta h_{j\\to i}|.$$ Substituting the second inequality into the first yields $$\\label{eq:component-difference}\n |\\Delta h_{j\\to i}|\n  \\le\\frac{|\\Delta h_j|+\\theta|\\Delta h_i|}{1-\\theta^2}.$$ In particular, on $[0,T]$ it is bounded by $H_T/(1-\\theta)$; Minkowski’s inequality and invariance also give $\\|\\Delta h_{j\\to i}\\|_2\\le\\sqrt V/(1-\\theta)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-236.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_definition": "The coefficients in this equation are products along directed paths. We first control the difference of one factor. For a directed edge $e=(i,j)$ define $$\\label{eq:log-discrepancy-def}\n D_e(t)=\\log a(h_{j\\to i}(t))-\n        \\log a(h'_{j\\to i}(t)),\n \\qquad S(t)=\\mathbb E[D_e(t)^2].$$ Transitivity on directed edges makes $S$ independent of $e$. The two cavity identities at the ends of $ij$ give $$|\\Delta h_{j\\to i}|\n     \\le|\\Delta h_j|+\\theta|\\Delta h_{i\\to j}|,\n \\qquad\n |\\Delta h_{i\\to j}|\n     \\le|\\Delta h_i|+\\theta|\\Delta h_{j\\to i}|.$$ Substituting the second inequality into the first yields $$\\label{eq:component-difference}\n |\\Delta h_{j\\to i}|\n  \\le\\frac{|\\Delta h_j|+\\theta|\\Delta h_i|}{1-\\theta^2}.$$ In particular, on $[0,T]$ it is bounded by $H_T/(1-\\theta)$; Minkowski’s inequality and invariance also give $\\|\\Delta h_{j\\to i}\\|_2\\le\\sqrt V/(1-\\theta)$.",
      "interpretation": "Difference of log response factors on a directed edge between the two copies, and its second moment.",
      "kind": "definition",
